# Manual fetch queue

Files the agents cannot download from muktedir (a Cloudflare or bot challenge, a 403 to every route, a login Firat holds), but a person in a normal browser can. Firat downloads them on the Mac and copies them to muktedir; a lane then stores and loads them.

## For Firat

1. Pick rows with status `open`. Open the page, download the files the "Get" column names, and keep the original filenames.
2. Copy each row's files into its own folder on muktedir, named by the row's ID:

   ```bash
   scp -r ~/Downloads/MF-001 muktedir:electionsarchive/data/inbox/manual/
   ```

   (Or put several folders in one directory and run `rsync -av ~/Downloads/manual/ muktedir:electionsarchive/data/inbox/manual/`.)

3. Change the row's status to `delivered` (or tell the coordinator), and add a line in Notes if the page differed from the description. If a file is not there, write `not found` in Notes.

## Mac session with Claude in Chrome

Agents start the Mac session themselves; nobody needs to paste a prompt. On muktedir, queue a job with `infra/macq.py`:

```bash
python3 infra/macq.py add --rows MF-013,MF-014 --by missing-elections
```

`add` prints a job ID. With no `--rows` and no message it takes every `open` row. Pass row IDs as one comma-separated list: words after the flags go to the Mac session as a note (for example a known mirror to try), so an ID written there is never marked. A row can sit in only one queued or running job.

Every job runs on the `claudePersonal` provider (the account Claude in Chrome on the Mac is signed in to) with `claude-sonnet-5-5`, whatever provider and model queued it. Pass `--model claude-opus-5-5` only for a row that already failed on Sonnet because finding the file took real searching. Any agent can queue: a T3 thread, a Claude Code desktop session on muktedir, or Firat by hand. Arm `macq.py wait {job}` right after `add`; that is how you get the result. Run `add` from a T3 session's own shell, not a detached or `nohup` one, and `finish` also sends that thread the result with `t3 thread send` (a busy thread gets it as its next turn). Other sessions get it only from `wait`.

`EA Downloads`, a background app on the Mac (launch agent `com.firatciftci.ea-macq`), checks the queue over ssh every few seconds, runs up to three jobs at once, and starts a T3 Code thread for each with `t3 thread start --wait` in the Mac's `EA Downloads` project (`~/.local/share/ea-macq/t3`), on the job's provider and model. The thread snoozes itself first and settles when it finishes. `~/.local/bin/claude-as` on the Mac turns Claude in Chrome on for that folder (`CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_CFC=1`), and the project's `.mcp.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json`, which `macq-install.sh` writes, add the `ea-macq` tools and the incoming folder and deny shell and file edits. The session drives a Chrome profile kept for this work, through Claude in Chrome. The app launches Chrome on that profile when it isn't running and hides Chrome before each job, so nothing shows on screen (its directory name is in `~/.config/ea-macq/chrome-profile` on the Mac; headless Chrome does not work, because the extension never connects there), and the session selects the Mac's Chrome from `list_connected_browsers`, so it never touches another browser. That profile saves every download into `~/Downloads/ea-macq/incoming/`, which the jobs running at the same time share. The session has the browser and read-only file tools, and no shell: it ends its reply with a JSON report naming each file as saved there. When it finishes, the app moves those files into `~/Downloads/ea-macq/{ID}/` and, when no other job is running, sets aside anything unreported in `~/Downloads/ea-macq/unclaimed/{job}/`; it then copies each delivered row's folder to `data/inbox/manual/{ID}/` and calls `macq.py finish`. `finish` checks that the folder arrived, then sets each row's status (`delivered`, `not found`, `unreachable`, or `needs Firat`) and appends a dated note naming the job. Do not commit those status edits; the lane commits them when it stores the files.

- `python3 infra/macq.py status` lists recent jobs and when the Mac last checked in. `status {job}` prints one job in full, including the session's report and its Mac thread.
- `python3 infra/macq.py cancel {job}` drops a queued job.
- Jobs wait while the Mac is asleep or offline, and run when it comes back. A job that crashes mid-run is put back in the queue when the app restarts or after 3 hours. A job that finds Chrome disconnected fails with no row changes; queue it again later.
- When a page shows a CAPTCHA, a Turnstile, or a login, the session asks Firat itself: it brings the hidden Chrome to the front on that page, posts a notification, and checks the page every 30 seconds for up to 15 minutes, then hides Chrome and carries on. Queue such rows like any other; never ask Firat in chat to clear a challenge for a job. A row the session marks `needs Firat` means he did not clear it in time (or a PDF opened in the viewer); it waits for him to download it by hand, following "For Firat" above. Do not queue it again unless he asks.
- When every job fails with "Chrome is not connected to Claude in Chrome", the extension in the jobs' Chrome profile is not signed in to the account of the provider the jobs run on. Tell Firat which provider the failing jobs used.
- When a job fails with "Chrome is not connected to Claude in Chrome", the dispatcher has already restarted Chrome and retried once; the extension sometimes drops its connection while Chrome keeps running. Queue the job again; tell Firat only if it fails twice in a row after a requeue.
- A row marked `unreachable` hit a site that did not load from the Mac (a timeout, a connection error, or a dead page). Firat cannot help with that: check the site from muktedir, try Wayback or another official mirror, and set the row back to `open` and queue it again once the site answers. Never bring an `unreachable` row to Firat, and never ask him to grant the extension access to a site; the Claude profile can already act on any site.
- Mac logs: `~/Library/Logs/ea-macq/` (per job, `{job}.t3.json` from `t3`, `{job}.json` as sent to `finish`, and `{job}.err`, plus `dispatch.log`); the full session is the job's thread in T3 Code on the Mac. Reinstall the app with `infra/macq-install.sh` on the Mac after changing `macq-dispatch.sh` or `macq-app/`.

## For agents

- Add a row when a source is blocked for real: the plain fetch, Wayback (`if_` and CDX), and the headless browser (`playwright` MCP) were all tried, and the failure is a challenge, a login, or a 403 on every route. Give the exact page URL, what to download, and the lane. Use the next free ID. Never ask Firat to get past a CAPTCHA or bot check for you on terms the site forbids; a page a person can open normally is fine.
- Then queue it for the Mac (above). When a row is `delivered`, read the files from `data/inbox/manual/{ID}/`, store each with `raw.store(source, url, content, ...)` using the page or file URL from the row and Firat's download date as `retrieved_at`, then load it through the normal parse, emit, apply, and publish path. Set the row to `stored` with the commit, and leave the inbox folder in place (it is git-ignored and lives only on muktedir).
- Note in the result set's methodology that the file was saved from a browser by a person, as the Tennessee 2025 file was.
